It has been really hard to enjoy anything when I feel this heavy.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety and worry about the future, or your feelings of sadness and hopelessness, are more prominent and troubling to you right now?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it is really hard to separate the two because I feel a combination of both. I am constantly dealing with persistent feelings of sadness and hopelessness, but at the same exact time, I have this non-stop worry and anxiety about my future. They both feel so overwhelming that I can't really say which one is worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms of sadness, hopelessness, and worry are directly related to the stress of your job transition and relocation, and would you expect them to improve if the situation with your job or living situation were to resolve or stabilize?\n\nPatient: Yes, I absolutely feel like my sadness and worry are directly tied to the stress of the relocation and job change. I really believe that once my living and job situations stabilize and I get more settled, these feelings will start to improve.\n\nDoctor: What do you think would help you the most right now to cope with this transition and start feeling better, and are you open to exploring different treatment options such as therapy or support groups?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm honestly not sure what would help the most right now, as it's hard to see a way out of this heavy feeling. I am definitely open to exploring therapy or other options, but I just don't know where to start.

I really believe that once my living and job situations stabilize and I get more settled, these feelings will start to improve.\n\nDoctor: What do you think would help you the most right now to cope with this transition and start feeling better, and are you open to exploring different treatment options such as therapy or support groups?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure what would help the most right now, as it's hard to see a way out of this heavy feeling. I am definitely open to exploring therapy or other options, but I just don't know where to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite, sleep patterns, or energy levels since you started feeling overwhelmed and down, and if so, how have they changed?"

I really believe that once my living and job situations stabilize and I get more settled, these feelings will start to improve.\n\nDoctor: What do you think would help you the most right now to cope with this transition and start feeling better, and are you open to exploring different treatment options such as therapy or support groups?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure what would help the most right now, as it's hard to see a way out of this heavy feeling. The patient reports persistent feelings of sadness and hopelessness, as well as non-stop worry and anxiety about the future, but does not mention significant changes in appetite, sleep, or energy levels, or experiences of guilt, worthlessness, or suicidal ideation that are typically seen in Major Depressive Disorder.
